# AI Media Scout — 2026-11-16

34 complete documentation guides and 56 additional screened creative-AI entries, including three GitLab-hosted projects. 9 earlier screened tools now have guides; these are completions, not new launch claims. Concrete workflows include editable type design, packaging vectors/folding previews, footage ingest, original tracker music, narrated film, multiview/4D reconstruction and shadow-puppet interaction. Every admission has a complete current software-grant review and six explicit quality questions; none was installed or run. All newly admitted entries remain Quality unverified or specifically cited Experimental. This Monday’s source review covers all 2919 previously published entries across every tier/platform/source host, with archived current sources and actual failed attempts. Nine existing labels change: six stale/unsupported Experimental verdicts move to Quality unverified, and three receive current specific Experimental reasons. The six higher-confidence entries retain their scoped labels after deeper current checks. Unavailable sources reopen affected checks to unknown; review coverage is not quality approval. Current restrictions/closed-source boundaries are disclosed without rewriting historical editions. The full effective discovery plan covers 107 fields, 1036 repository queries and 25 model tasks, including a new shadow-performance field, adaptive terms and deeper/date-partitioned pagination. Live primary research supplements every field and all eight configured ecosystems. Metadata-level searches covered the complete current and retained discovery catalogs, including historical pilot records, followed by targeted document/license/code reviews beyond the README shortlist. Remaining raw unscreened and excluded candidates stay local. Eight complete research PDFs were captured; selected abstracts/front matter were read for field context, not claimed as full-paper quality review. Direct GitLab raw URLs and older topic sorting had 429 gaps, while official APIs and a latest-activity listing supplied current project evidence. Codeberg searches remained throttled/robots-limited and some project/manual URLs failed. Exact attempt/bounded-source counts follow from verified collection. No star floor, fixed admission quota, platform preference, app execution or additional scheduler is used. Verified collection: 1036/1036 repository queries attempted; 1033 successful, 3 partial, 0 failed and 137 bounded/truncated. All 25 model tasks were attempted; 0 failed. These are bounded discovery attempts, not an exhaustive census or quality approval. Empty model-source responses: text2text-generation. An empty response does not establish that these creative workflows have no tools. Separate from successful search queries, 52 watchlist fetches and 7 enrichment attempts failed; the detailed warnings remain in the report. A 404/409 is an availability gap, not proof of abandonment or license revocation.

Documentation reviewed today. Tools are not hands-on tested unless explicitly stated. Requirements and performance remain source-specific.

| Neural hair, fur and strand-groom authoring | HairGS, IPHG, StrandHead and HairGPT pages provide generation/reconstruction context. Digital Salon’s new guide additionally separates learned hairstyle retrieval, physics grooming, optional diffusion rendering and the licensed data/mesh that are not fully bundled. [Source 1](https://github.com/yimin-pan/hair-gs) [Source 2](https://xiaokunsun.github.io/StrandHead.github.io/) [Source 3](https://haiminluo.github.io/hairgpt/) [Source 4](https://github.com/facebookresearch/iphg) [Source 5](https://digital-salon.github.io/) |
| Neural smoke, fluid capture and editable VFX fields | HyFluid’s author page and the PICT_smoke repository were checked; the separate PICT project-site URL returns 404. Today’s direct primary-source attempts include 1 unavailable/access-limited source; affected claims are not newly verified from those failed responses. [Source 1](https://kovenyu.com/HyFluid/) [Source 2](https://19reborn.github.io/PICT_smoke/) [Source 3](https://github.com/19reborn/PICT_smoke) |
| AI pattern transcription and symbolic craft verification | StitchProof and Starlit connect model-assisted interpretation with deterministic pattern/count checking. A successful count check does not establish a physically correct garment or reliable model instruction reading. [Source 1](https://huggingface.co/Qwen/Qwen3.5-4B) [Source 2](https://stitchfoundry.github.io/StitchFoundry/) |
| Neural rhythm tracking and synchronized visual media | The BeatNet paper, Rust package documentation and registry were rechecked. These provide neural rhythm context, while the current LyricsSync guide documents a separate Beat This!-based timing and manual-correction workflow. Separate software/checkpoint terms remain visible. [Source 1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2108.03576) [Source 2](https://docs.rs/crate/beatnet-rs/latest/source/README.md) |
| Foundation-model dioramas and compositional scene assembly | Diorama’s author page and current released code describe foundation-model perception, CAD retrieval and scene-layout optimization from an image. The guide preserves the multi-stage setup and model/CAD/service gaps; imagined hidden geometry is not measured ground truth. [Source 1](https://3dlg-hcvc.github.io/diorama/) |
| AI planner layouts, book arts and binding-aware publishing | Heritage Studio, eManuSkript and HornPenguin Booklet provide live book-layout/manuscript context, but conventional imposition alone is not AI and no new complete software grant is established for the unresolved layout candidates. BoxCreator’s reviewed MIT agent workflow adds editable packaging dielines and folding previews with explicit print/physical-proof limits. [Source 1](https://zenodo.org/records/18999747) [Source 2](https://www.rosette.dev/docs) [Source 3](https://jonidimo.github.io/agenda-heritage-generator/) [Source 4](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/jonidimo/agenda-heritage-generator/main/README.md) [Source 5](https://github.com/baojiachen0214/BoxCreator) |
| AI shadow puppetry and hand-shadow performance | Live sources distinguish local speech/LLM shadow-puppet interaction in ShadowLite from an agent-authored shadow visual style in BB Video. HaSPeR’s correct CVF paper describes a recognition dataset and teaching prototype; its current root MIT grant conflicts in stated scope with a CC-BY-NC-SA README notice, so no application is admitted pending clarification. Sketchry SHADOWPLAY is free/account-gated in its current page but no open-source grant was established, so free access is not treated as OSS eligibility. The CUNY virtual-Wayang page is proposal context, not proof of a working released system. [Source 1](https://github.com/QuentinCrane/ShadowLite) [Source 2](https://github.com/BubbleBrainBB/BB-Video) [Source 3](https://openaccess.thecvf.com/content/ICCV2025W/WCCA/papers/Raiyan_HaSPeR_An_Image_Repository_for_Hand_Shadow_Puppet_Recognition_ICCVW_2025_paper.pdf) [Source 4](https://sketchry.app/skills/shadowplay/) [Source 5](https://github.com/Starscream-11813/HaSPeR/blob/main/README.md) [Source 6](https://github.com/Starscream-11813/HaSPeR/blob/main/LICENSE) |

## Search and review counts

107 creative fields; 1036/1036 repository queries attempted; 25/25 model-task queries attempted. 78398 distinct source candidates and 1074 model leads. 34 detailed profiles and 56 additional screened discoveries. Source gaps: 0 failed repository queries, 3 partial repository queries, 137 bounded repository queries, 0 failed model-task queries and 0 web ecosystem gaps. Raw search candidates include duplicates of known tools, excluded projects and projects awaiting review; they are not verified recommendations.

## Research beyond GitHub

- **gitlab** · searched: The live latest-activity video-generation listing and official public APIs supplied three screened projects: UmeAiRT ComfyUI graphs, Brainrotten's original-media pipeline and the Agent Motion plugin. Complete MIT grants and actual graph/agent/render source were reviewed. Earlier raw URLs and other topic sorting returned429; those failures remain, and Brainrotten's third-party cartoon/voice material is not licensed by MIT. This is bounded host research, not a full GitLab census. [Source](https://gitlab.com/explore/projects/topics/video-generation?sort=latest_activity_desc) [Source](https://gitlab.com/UmeAiRT-Studio/comfyui-workflows) [Source](https://gitlab.com/wdipak/brainrotten) [Source](https://gitlab.com/VRymarovich/agent-motion-plugin)
- **codeberg** · searched: Imaginer and Raspberry Pi diffusion metadata/pages were revisited, but current full documentation/grant routes met bot challenges. Diffusion/audio searches returned429 and web crawling was robots-limited. These are dated access gaps; current licensing/setup checks stay unknown rather than inferred from retained metadata or historical grants. [Source](https://codeberg.org/Imaginer/Imaginer) [Source](https://codeberg.org/MightyOwlbear/Raspberry-Pi-5-Diffusion) [Source](https://codeberg.org/api/v1/repos/Imaginer/Imaginer) [Source](https://codeberg.org/api/v1/repos/search?q=diffusion&limit=25) [Source](https://codeberg.org/api/v1/repos/search?q=audio&limit=25)
- **sourcehut** · searched: The live diffuser source/license/manifest were revisited and its complete MIT grant was read. The Cargo manifest's absolute author-local dependency path remains a failed portable-setup check. Search crawling was robots-limited; the accessible project source is a concrete finding, not evidence that all SourceHut creative tools were searched or that this workflow was reproduced. [Source](https://sr.ht/projects?search=diffusion) [Source](https://git.sr.ht/~rockerboo/diffuser/blob/main/Cargo.toml) [Source](https://git.sr.ht/~rockerboo/diffuser/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **packages** · searched: Current npm/PyPI/NuGet/Rust metadata supplements source discovery without installation. TensorStack.Media's index lists0.14.13; mcp-score-server describes an agent/music21/MuseScore route with Python3.14+. The beatnet-rs registry's CC-BY4 label is not assumed to be a standard software grant. PyClef still lacks a complete reviewed software grant; a listing alone does not admit it. Geniartor is classical heuristic-search composition, not a neural model. [Source](https://api.nuget.org/v3-flatcontainer/tensorstack.media/index.json) [Source](https://pypi.org/pypi/mcp-score-server/json) [Source](https://pypi.org/pypi/pyclef/json) [Source](https://crates.io/api/v1/crates/beatnet-rs) [Source](https://pypi.org/pypi/geniartor/json)
- **creative-plugins** · searched: Primary Krita/REAPER/SAT/Max-related sources and current plugin code supplement discovery. New GitLab Agent Motion supports an AI host integrating editable Remotion source; UmeAiRT graphs require the matching custom-node ecosystem. Open plugin/skill code does not license the proprietary host, remote registry components or selected model. Optional alpha ports are separated from the assessed core workflow. [Source](https://github.com/Aiwist/ai-transcriber) [Source](https://docs.interstice.cloud/) [Source](https://suite.sat.qc.ca/en/tools/) [Source](https://sat-mtl.github.io/koaia/installation.html) [Source](https://gitlab.com/VRymarovich/agent-motion-plugin)
- **project-sites** · searched: Live primary captures supplement all107 effective fields, including newly added shadow performance. Named gaps include Illuminate timeout, KinoDraw/MapStory/PICT/Feature3DGS missing URLs, openslides TLS and an old manimvox525 route; alternate manimvox Pages documentation remains accessible. Eight complete research PDFs were archived; selected abstracts/front matter supply context, without full-paper quality-review claims. Sketchry SHADOWPLAY's free/account access did not establish an open-source grant. [Source](https://3dlg-hcvc.github.io/diorama/) [Source](https://digital-salon.github.io/) [Source](https://zenodo.org/records/18999747) [Source](https://sketchry.app/skills/shadowplay/) [Source](https://openaccess.thecvf.com/content/ICCV2025W/WCCA/papers/Raiyan_HaSPeR_An_Image_Repository_for_Hand_Shadow_Puppet_Recognition_ICCVW_2025_paper.pdf)
- **research-code** · searched: Released research code spans hair/scene construction, neural music/vision and capture. The earlier screened4DAnyone now has a concrete native4DGS reconstruction/viewer addition, explicitly different in quality/speed from the paper implementation. Independent HairDeck integration code supports a scoped use check while gated-model/dependency gaps remain. Research origin, papers and star counts do not establish a quality tier or grant model/data rights. [Source](https://github.com/ant-research/4DAnyone/blob/main/docs/4dgs.md) [Source](https://github.com/digital-salon/Digital-Salon) [Source](https://github.com/3dlg-hcvc/diorama) [Source](https://github.com/VisualBruno/ComfyUI-HairDeck) [Source](https://github.com/Nikolay-Lysenko/geniartor/blob/master/geniartor/optimization.py)
- **international** · searched: Current source review includes Chinese packaging/glyph/layout and shadow-puppet workflows, Hindi caption/short-video use, Korean lyric timing, right-to-left motion design and an Android AI album workflow. Chinese BoxCreator distinguishes editable design/geometry from physical printing approval; Mori distinguishes open organizer code from opaque separately supplied scraper/runtime objects. Language and popularity do not imply quality or platform support. [Source](https://github.com/baojiachen0214/BoxCreator) [Source](https://github.com/QuentinCrane/ShadowLite) [Source](https://github.com/EonGreen0/Mori-Android-AI) [Source](https://github.com/Ismail-Dilshad/tempoloom) [Source](https://github.com/elecpure2/LyricsSync) [Source](https://github.com/zhang-yunqi/TextDiff)

Scope: Developing original Latin wordmarks and font families, inspecting spacing and kerning, or turning authorized print handwriting into static fonts with editable UFO sources.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-16. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/byrishi/punchcutter/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The maintainer supplies five-family specimens, Studio screenshots, a prebuilt Pomelo Round specimen and photo-to-font examples. The demo handwriting is explicitly simulated. These document the workflow without independently establishing professional font quality. [Source](https://github.com/byrishi/punchcutter/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://byrishi.github.io/punchcutter/getting-started.html)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Use Python 3.10+ and a supported coding agent or MCP client. The documented MCP package installs from the official Git repository; register the resulting punchcutter mcp command in the chosen client. On macOS, proof images and handwriting sheets additionally need Cairo. Agent-host setup differs by client. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/byrishi/punchcutter/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://byrishi.github.io/punchcutter/getting-started.html)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** Inspected the titles/states in up to ten recently updated issue/PR records and selected first-hand report bodies; 0 records returned. This bounded sample, owner PRs and repository activity do not establish sustained support, closed blockers or independent reliability. [Source](https://github.com/byrishi/punchcutter/issues)
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** Toolkit code is MIT. Example families and open-base remixes retain SIL OFL 1.1; generated original families follow the creator-selected terms. AI-host services and rights to supplied handwriting/reference images remain separate. Local Python computation plus any chosen AI-agent subscription/API charges. No universal zero-cost AI-host claim is established. [Source](https://github.com/byrishi/punchcutter/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://byrishi.github.io/punchcutter/getting-started.html)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Drawn output is Latin-only, upright sans/slab; slant is oblique, not a true italic. Handwriting is print-style/static, not joined cursive. Outlines are unhinted, and a type designer still needs to refine curves/kerning. Anonymous telemetry is documented with an opt-out. No app was run.

### How it uses AI

An agent-facing type-design toolkit lets a visual AI turn a brief or handwriting reference into parameter changes, inspect proof images, critique glyphs and rebuild an editable font family. Its font compiler is deterministic; the AI design/critique loop is the creative integration. [Source](https://github.com/byrishi/punchcutter/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://byrishi.github.io/punchcutter/getting-started.html)

### Introduction

An agent-facing type-design toolkit lets a visual AI turn a brief or handwriting reference into parameter changes, inspect proof images, critique glyphs and rebuild an editable font family. Its font compiler is deterministic; the AI design/critique loop is the creative integration. ### What it is good for

Developing original Latin wordmarks and font families, inspecting spacing and kerning, or turning authorized print handwriting into static fonts with editable UFO sources. These document the workflow without independently establishing professional font quality. [Source 1](https://github.com/byrishi/punchcutter/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://byrishi.github.io/punchcutter/getting-started.html)

### Install

Use Python 3.10+ and a supported coding agent or MCP client. The documented MCP package installs from the official Git repository; register the resulting punchcutter mcp command in the chosen client. On macOS, proof images and handwriting sheets additionally need Cairo. Agent-host setup differs by client. [Source 1](https://github.com/byrishi/punchcutter/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://byrishi.github.io/punchcutter/getting-started.html)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Use Python 3.10+ and a supported coding agent or MCP client. The documented MCP package installs from the official Git repository; register the resulting punchcutter mcp command in the chosen client. On macOS, proof images and handwriting sheets additionally need Cairo. Agent-host setup differs by client.

```sh
pip install "punchcutter[mcp] @ git+https://github.com/byrishi/punchcutter"
```


```sh
punchcutter mcp
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/byrishi/punchcutter/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://byrishi.github.io/punchcutter/getting-started.html)

1. Ask for an original family with a name, mood and intended use; select a drawn, handwriting or open-base route.
2. Inspect proof sheets and individual glyphs, then ask the agent to revise curves, weight, spacing or kerning.
3. Build the family and inspect the QA report; open the generated Studio to test words and language coverage.
4. Keep the UFO/design sources and export only after human typographic review. Preserve OFL notices and renamed-family requirements for open-font remixes.

- **Software:** Python 3.10+; package dependencies; an image-capable agent for visual critique, or the deterministic CLI; Cairo on macOS for proofs/sheets.
- **Platforms:** Python and client routes are documented; font-install instructions cover Mac, Windows and Linux. This does not independently verify every client/OS combination.

## Unableton

Audio, music & voice

The repository was created on 2026-11-14. This is its first full guide in this library, not independent validation of a new release.

### Quality assessment: Experimental

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Sketching editable arrangements through chat, correcting notes in a piano roll and exporting stereo WAV or multitrack MIDI on a supported Mac.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-16. No local execution implied.

Why Experimental: The current application is explicitly a development preview; signed/notarized distribution and accessibility/platform acceptance remain unfinished for the assessed music-editor workflow. https://github.com/VirajShah21/unableton/blob/main/README.md https://github.com/VirajShah21/unableton/blob/main/docs/user-guide.md

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/VirajShah21/unableton/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The screenshots show a locally prepared arrangement and an unsent refinement prompt, explicitly not a recorded AI-generation run. The documented draft/validate/commit diagram is evidence of intended behavior, not independently verified musical results. [Source](https://github.com/VirajShah21/unableton/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/VirajShah21/unableton/blob/main/docs/user-guide.md)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Obtain the official Apple Silicon development-preview DMG for macOS 26+. Source builds additionally need Swift 6.2+ and Xcode command-line tools; the published build-and-run script builds and ad-hoc signs the app. In Settings, supply an OpenAI key, test the connection and choose a fixed model or the Automatic routing mode. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/VirajShah21/unableton/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/VirajShah21/unableton/blob/main/docs/user-guide.md)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** Inspected the titles/states in up to ten recently updated issue/PR records and selected first-hand report bodies; 0 records returned. This bounded sample, owner PRs and repository activity do not establish sustained support, closed blockers or independent reliability. [Source](https://github.com/virajshah21/unableton/issues)
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** Application code is Apache-2.0. OpenAI composition, optional vocal services/Jev review and downloaded FreePats banks retain their own terms. No sampled banks or demo audio are bundled. Rinoa integration terms are explicitly unresolved. Manual editing, built-in playback and single-layer faithful local transcription can run offline; cloud composition uses the user’s separately billed OpenAI account. Optional services have separate access and billing. [Source](https://github.com/VirajShah21/unableton/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/VirajShah21/unableton/blob/main/docs/user-guide.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Development preview: signing/notarization, complete VoiceOver acceptance and macOS 27 validation remain unfinished. One project plays/records at a time. Local detection is monophonic, does not transcribe lyrics and can err. Third-party plugins, external MIDI recording, advanced time stretching and comping are outside this release. Default credential storage is an owner-only unencrypted local file; Keychain is an explicit option.

### How it uses AI

A native music editor converts cloud-LLM requests into typed edits of tracks, MIDI-like notes, arrangements and instrument settings. An AI edit is committed as one undoable project change; manual music editing and local playback remain available. [Source](https://github.com/VirajShah21/unableton/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/VirajShah21/unableton/blob/main/docs/user-guide.md)

### Install

Obtain the official Apple Silicon development-preview DMG for macOS 26+. Source builds additionally need Swift 6.2+ and Xcode command-line tools; the published build-and-run script builds and ad-hoc signs the app. In Settings, supply an OpenAI key, test the connection and choose a fixed model or the Automatic routing mode. [Source 1](https://github.com/VirajShah21/unableton/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/VirajShah21/unableton/blob/main/docs/user-guide.md)

2. Obtain the official Apple Silicon development-preview DMG for macOS 26+. Source builds additionally need Swift 6.2+ and Xcode command-line tools; the published build-and-run script builds and ad-hoc signs the app. In Settings, supply an OpenAI key, test the connection and choose a fixed model or the Automatic routing mode.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/VirajShah21/unableton.git
```


```sh
cd unableton
```


```sh
./script/build_and_run.sh
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/VirajShah21/unableton/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/VirajShah21/unableton/blob/main/docs/user-guide.md)

1. Start a song and request a short arrangement; keep the first request small and inspect every track.
2. Select a track or clip for context, refine notes, velocities and sounds, and use Command-Z to undo a whole AI edit.
3. For performance input, open Transcribe, record/import a monophonic passage, preview detected events and explicitly Add to Song.
4. Save the .unableton package and verify WAV/MIDI exports. Download only the FreePats banks actually used; optional vocals need separate consent and provider terms.

## Vertigo

Images & design · 3D, reconstruction & assets · Video, animation & film

First complete guide in this library, including completion of any earlier screened entry; not a claim of a launch today.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Turning a permitted photograph into a short parallax/dolly-zoom concept loop or a miniature diorama shot, with human review of foreground edges and exposed background areas.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-16. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/chris-wozniczek/vertigo/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The official static demo and forest example document the intended interface; bundled samples have precomputed depth. They do not demonstrate independent fresh-photo inference or eliminate depth/occlusion artifacts. [Source](https://github.com/chris-wozniczek/vertigo/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://chris-wozniczek.github.io/vertigo/)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Use the official browser demo, or clone the MIT source, install its npm dependencies and start the Vite development server. A fresh photo requires a first model download; WebGPU fp16 is used when available, otherwise WASM q8. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/chris-wozniczek/vertigo/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://chris-wozniczek.github.io/vertigo/)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** Inspected the titles/states in up to ten recently updated issue/PR records and selected first-hand report bodies; 0 records returned. This bounded sample, owner PRs and repository activity do not establish sustained support, closed blockers or independent reliability. [Source](https://github.com/chris-wozniczek/vertigo/issues)
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** MIT application code. The named ONNX Depth Anything V2 Small conversion has separate model terms/provenance; Three.js, Transformers.js, fonts and Unsplash sample-photo rights remain separate. Local browser compute and initial model/static-asset downloads; no paid inference service is documented for this selected path. [Source](https://github.com/chris-wozniczek/vertigo/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://chris-wozniczek.github.io/vertigo/)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. This creates a two-plane depth effect, not a complete recovered 3D scene. Background filling is deterministic push–pull processing; inferred depth and hidden surfaces need visual review. Samples can skip model inference because depth is precomputed. Network requests still include the site, fonts and model weights even though photo pixels are processed locally.

### How it uses AI

Depth Anything V2 Small estimates a depth map in a browser worker. Two depth-displaced image planes and deterministic background filling then support looping camera motion and relighting of a still photograph. [Source](https://github.com/chris-wozniczek/vertigo/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://chris-wozniczek.github.io/vertigo/)

### Introduction

Depth Anything V2 Small estimates a depth map in a browser worker. Two depth-displaced image planes and deterministic background filling then support looping camera motion and relighting of a still photograph. [Source 1](https://github.com/chris-wozniczek/vertigo/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://chris-wozniczek.github.io/vertigo/)

### What it is good for

Turning a permitted photograph into a short parallax/dolly-zoom concept loop or a miniature diorama shot, with human review of foreground edges and exposed background areas. [Source 1](https://github.com/chris-wozniczek/vertigo/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://chris-wozniczek.github.io/vertigo/)

### Demo & examples

The official static demo and forest example document the intended interface; bundled samples have precomputed depth. They do not demonstrate independent fresh-photo inference or eliminate depth/occlusion artifacts. [Source 1](https://github.com/chris-wozniczek/vertigo/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://chris-wozniczek.github.io/vertigo/)

### Install

Use the official browser demo, or clone the MIT source, install its npm dependencies and start the Vite development server. A fresh photo requires a first model download; WebGPU fp16 is used when available, otherwise WASM q8. [Source 1](https://github.com/chris-wozniczek/vertigo/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://chris-wozniczek.github.io/vertigo/)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Use the official browser demo, or clone the MIT source, install its npm dependencies and start the Vite development server. A fresh photo requires a first model download; WebGPU fp16 is used when available, otherwise WASM q8.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/chris-wozniczek/vertigo.git
```


```sh
cd vertigo
```


```sh
npm install
```


```sh
npm run dev
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/chris-wozniczek/vertigo/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://chris-wozniczek.github.io/vertigo/)

1. Drop/paste a permitted photo or choose a sample and wait for depth estimation.
2. Select Vertigo, Ken Burns, Orbit, Crane, Swing or Window; adjust depth intensity, haze, light, focus and grade.
3. Inspect depth boundaries throughout the camera move, especially newly exposed background regions.
4. Export a documented 4–6-second loop in 16:9, square or 9:16 at 1080p, or a PNG. MP4/H.264 availability depends on browser MediaRecorder support; WebM is the fallback.

- **Software:** A modern browser with WebGPU or the documented WASM fallback; Node/npm for source development. Explicit minimum Node version is not documented.
- **Platforms:** Browser workflow and phone tilt are documented. Desktop OS/version support and uniform mobile export compatibility are not independently established.

### How it uses AI

A Windows wallpaper/editor application documents local learned depth estimation, reference-image style transfer and super-resolution, alongside conventional layers, particle atmospheres and Web wallpaper authoring. [Source](https://github.com/PaperHammer/VirtualPaper/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/PaperHammer/VirtualPaper/wiki)

### Introduction

A Windows wallpaper/editor application documents local learned depth estimation, reference-image style transfer and super-resolution, alongside conventional layers, particle atmospheres and Web wallpaper authoring. [Source 1](https://github.com/PaperHammer/VirtualPaper/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/PaperHammer/VirtualPaper/wiki)

### What it is good for

Preparing locally enhanced stills, depth-parallax desktop artwork and editable image/Web wallpaper projects across one or several Windows displays. [Source 1](https://github.com/PaperHammer/VirtualPaper/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/PaperHammer/VirtualPaper/wiki)

### Demo & examples

The README supplies synchronized regular/depth parallax, a source/style/result triptych and a downsampled 960×540 input enlarged to 4K with matched bicubic/model crops. These are maintainer examples, not an independent restoration-quality test. [Source 1](https://github.com/PaperHammer/VirtualPaper/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/PaperHammer/VirtualPaper/wiki)

### Install

Download the official x64 installer for Windows 10 version 2004/build 19041+ or Windows 11. The installer checks .NET 8 Desktop Runtime and can fetch it; Edge WebView2 Runtime is also required. Launch VirtualPaper and add a small image to its library before testing enhancement. [Source 1](https://github.com/PaperHammer/VirtualPaper/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/PaperHammer/VirtualPaper/wiki)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Download the official x64 installer for Windows 10 version 2004/build 19041+ or Windows 11. The installer checks .NET 8 Desktop Runtime and can fetch it; Edge WebView2 Runtime is also required. Launch VirtualPaper and add a small image to its library before testing enhancement.
### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/PaperHammer/VirtualPaper/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/PaperHammer/VirtualPaper/wiki)

1. Import a permitted image, preview its fit/crop and retain the original.
2. Try local style transfer, super-resolution or depth parallax on a small representative image; compare fine structure and edges.
3. Adjust parallax/atmosphere and choose separate, duplicated or stretched multi-display playback.
4. Save editable .vpd/.vpw projects; export a supported image or Web wallpaper package, and check the actual display/export result.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/PaperHammer/VirtualPaper/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/PaperHammer/VirtualPaper/wiki)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Windows x64; .NET 8 Desktop Runtime and Edge WebView2 Runtime. Source-development and elevated acceptance-test instructions are separate from ordinary installer use.
- **Platforms:** Windows 10 version 2004/build 19041 or newer and Windows 11 x64. Mac/Linux support is not documented.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/PaperHammer/VirtualPaper/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/PaperHammer/VirtualPaper/blob/main/README.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/PaperHammer/VirtualPaper/wiki)

- **Code:** GPL-3.0
- **Weights:** The complete GPL-3.0 software grant was compared with the full standard; only an explanatory GNU URL differs. Model weights and other bundled components retain separate terms, which this review does not clear universally.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows GPL-3.0. The complete GPL-3.0 software grant was compared with the full standard; only an explanatory GNU URL differs. Model weights and other bundled components retain separate terms, which this review does not clear universally.
- **Cost:** No paid inference service is documented for the selected local AI operations. Local computation, runtimes and model/dependency downloads remain required.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/PaperHammer/VirtualPaper/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/PaperHammer/VirtualPaper/wiki)

### Limitations

Depth motion is an inferred parallax effect, not recovered hidden geometry. Hardware-dependent processing speed and enhancement artifacts need review. Model-specific memory requirements, model identities/terms and sustained independent support remain incomplete in this profile. [Source 1](https://github.com/PaperHammer/VirtualPaper/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/PaperHammer/VirtualPaper/wiki)

## EggLab

Images & design

The repository was created on 2026-11-15. This is its first full guide in this library, not independent validation of a new release.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Generating draft images, multi-reference compositions and cutouts beside a Windows PhotoCraft document, with document-scoped conversations and explicit layer placement.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-16. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/YanNyeinHtet/EggLab/blob/master/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The official panel/setup/session screenshots document the workflow. Current owner measurements report 29s versus47s for Q4_K_M versusFP8 Turbo text-to-image on one RTX4060Ti8 GB at864x1152; these are owner measurements, not independent quality or universal speed guarantees. Quantization does not remove the four-reference workspace limit. [Source](https://github.com/YanNyeinHtet/EggLab/blob/master/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/YanNyeinHtet/EggLab/blob/master/PERFORMANCE-RESEARCH.md)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Install PhotoCraft separately, then obtain the official Windows x64 EggLab installer. Its wizard detects PhotoCraft, OpenCode and the image engine, then downloads/imports the selected models. Configure the agent model through OpenCode providers; Node 20+ is needed only for source development. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/YanNyeinHtet/EggLab/blob/master/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/YanNyeinHtet/EggLab/blob/master/PERFORMANCE-RESEARCH.md)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** Inspected the titles/states in up to ten recently updated issue/PR records and selected first-hand report bodies; 0 records returned. This bounded sample, owner PRs and repository activity do not establish sustained support, closed blockers or independent reliability. [Source](https://github.com/yannyeinhtet/egglab/issues)
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** MIT EggLab code. PhotoCraft host rights/cost and OpenCode/provider terms are separate. The selected Qwen model revision, text encoder, VAE and LoRA need their own terms; no blanket model permission follows from MIT. Local image compute plus any PhotoCraft/agent/provider access and fees. The README describes free and paid agent choices, without establishing a perpetual free-service entitlement. [Source](https://github.com/YanNyeinHtet/EggLab/blob/master/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/YanNyeinHtet/EggLab/blob/master/PERFORMANCE-RESEARCH.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Image inference stays local, but chat text goes to the selected model provider. Multi-reference edits compete heavily for VRAM; automatic retries may reduce dimensions. Current installers are unsigned. Documented source/installer workflows were not executed by this research.

### How it uses AI

A local stable-diffusion.cpp image engine is connected to an OpenCode design agent and the separate PhotoCraft canvas, so generated and reference-edited images can be placed as editable document layers. [Source](https://github.com/YanNyeinHtet/EggLab/blob/master/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/YanNyeinHtet/EggLab/blob/master/PERFORMANCE-RESEARCH.md)

### Install

Install PhotoCraft separately, then obtain the official Windows x64 EggLab installer. Its wizard detects PhotoCraft, OpenCode and the image engine, then downloads/imports the selected models. 1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Install PhotoCraft separately, then obtain the official Windows x64 EggLab installer. Its wizard detects PhotoCraft, OpenCode and the image engine, then downloads/imports the selected models. Configure the agent model through OpenCode providers; Node 20+ is needed only for source development.

```sh
cd panel

npm install

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/YanNyeinHtet/EggLab/blob/master/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/YanNyeinHtet/EggLab/blob/master/PERFORMANCE-RESEARCH.md)

2. Choose a chat provider and a small output size; begin a conversation attached to the current PhotoCraft document.
3. Generate or reference-edit a draft, inspect its job details and place the accepted result as a layer.
4. For memory failures, inspect encoder-on-CPU/reduced-output retries and lower output/reference size explicitly. Export and retain a layered project before further edits.

- **Platforms:** Windows 10 or 11 x64. Mac/Linux support is not documented. CUDA, Vulkan and CPU engine choices are documented with hardware-dependent behavior.

The catalog includes noncommercial, research-only, regional and gated models. An open-weight model is not automatically open source or commercially unrestricted. Local hardware, storage and initial model downloads. Optional cloud providers have separate keys, budgets and charges and are off by default. Runtime offline behavior requires prior model/environment downloads.

### How it uses AI

A local studio queues per-model inference workers for images, 3D assets and audio, with a shared asset library, model-specific recipes, a prompt improver and agent-facing MCP tools. [Source](https://github.com/utain/open-asset-studio/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/utain/open-asset-studio/blob/main/docs/SETUP.md)

Prepare Linux x86_64/aarch64, an NVIDIA driver supporting CUDA 12.8+, git, ffmpeg and uv. [Source 1](https://github.com/utain/open-asset-studio/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/utain/open-asset-studio/blob/main/docs/SETUP.md)

Start run_studio.sh on loopback, then inspect the Models page before submitting jobs.

git clone https://github.com/utain/open-asset-studio.git
```

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Use Python 3.9+, FFmpeg/ffprobe and whisper.cpp as whisper-cli. Obtain the documented ggml-base.en model and place it under models/ or configure its path. - **Models, services and costs · documented:** MIT engine code and bundled three.js; Whisper/conversion and optional Phonon-2/provider models retain separate terms. Hosted CutBot accounts/messaging/billing are outside this open engine. Local compute for the selected no-cloud-planner path. Optional OpenAI planning/b-roll/SFX/graphics are separately billed; graphics can require many model calls per scene. [Source](https://github.com/justinbuildsmov/cutbot-oss/blob/main/README.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Without whisper, only dead-air removal runs. Prompts are tuned for short vertical tech/talking-head speech. Music, stickers and aspect changes are unsupported; processing is sequential. Experimental AI graphics and unfinished 3D prompting do not determine the baseline editor’s whole-tool label. No local execution occurred.

### How it uses AI

Local whisper.cpp transcription splits a talking-head recording into speech runs; a heuristic or optional LLM then proposes retake cuts, followed by a cut-edge check and FFmpeg render. [Source](https://github.com/justinbuildsmov/cutbot-oss/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

Local whisper.cpp transcription splits a talking-head recording into speech runs; a heuristic or optional LLM then proposes retake cuts, followed by a cut-edge check and FFmpeg render. [Source 1](https://github.com/justinbuildsmov/cutbot-oss/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Preparing a reviewed talking-head rough cut, keeping deliberate repetition and inspecting whether soft word endings survive. The selected baseline excludes experimental generated graphics. [Source 1](https://github.com/justinbuildsmov/cutbot-oss/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The maintainer distinguishes hosted-service-used core operations, less-tested b-roll/SFX/Phonon routes and explicitly experimental graphics/3D. These are scoped author claims; no independent creative-output test was established. [Source 1](https://github.com/justinbuildsmov/cutbot-oss/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Use Python 3.9+, FFmpeg/ffprobe and whisper.cpp as whisper-cli. Obtain the documented ggml-base.en model and place it under models/ or configure its path. The Python core has no pip dependencies. Caption/face/graphics helpers additionally require macOS and Swift/Xcode command-line tools. [Source 1](https://github.com/justinbuildsmov/cutbot-oss/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Use Python 3.9+, FFmpeg/ffprobe and whisper.cpp as whisper-cli. Obtain the documented ggml-base.en model and place it under models/ or configure its path. The Python core has no pip dependencies. Caption/face/graphics helpers additionally require macOS and Swift/Xcode command-line tools.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/justinbuildsmov/cutbot-oss.git
```


```sh
cd cutbot-oss
```


```sh
python -m cutbot take.mov --no-ai --dump take.json
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/justinbuildsmov/cutbot-oss/blob/main/README.md)

1. Choose a short talking-head recording and retain the source. Run the no-cloud-planner baseline with transcription available.
2. Inspect the dumped decisions and listen at every cut, especially repeated lines, asides and soft endings.
3. If using the optional LLM planner, configure its provider privately and give an explicit editorial instruction; model calls incur separate access/cost.
4. Check the H.264/AAC output, caption timing if enabled and loudness. Add only authorized b-roll/SFX; their files are not bundled.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/justinbuildsmov/cutbot-oss/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Python 3.9+ standard library, FFmpeg/ffprobe, whisper.cpp and a separately downloaded ggml model. Swift/Xcode command-line tools on macOS for native helper features.
- **Platforms:** Only macOS testing is documented. The Python/FFmpeg core is described as portable, but this review does not infer tested Windows/Linux support. Native helper features require macOS.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/justinbuildsmov/cutbot-oss/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/justinbuildsmov/cutbot-oss/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** MIT engine code and bundled three.js; Whisper/conversion and optional Phonon-2/provider models retain separate terms. Hosted CutBot accounts/messaging/billing are outside this open engine.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. MIT engine code and bundled three.js; Whisper/conversion and optional Phonon-2/provider models retain separate terms. Hosted CutBot accounts/messaging/billing are outside this open engine.
- **Cost:** Local compute for the selected no-cloud-planner path. Optional OpenAI planning/b-roll/SFX/graphics are separately billed; graphics can require many model calls per scene.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/justinbuildsmov/cutbot-oss/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

Without whisper, only dead-air removal runs. Prompts are tuned for short vertical tech/talking-head speech. Music, stickers and aspect changes are unsupported; processing is sequential. Experimental AI graphics and unfinished 3D prompting do not determine the baseline editor’s whole-tool label. No local execution occurred. [Source 1](https://github.com/justinbuildsmov/cutbot-oss/blob/main/README.md)

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/mandalerajesh-byte/reelkit/blob/main/README.md)

1. Begin with a short authorized clip and an explicit language; retain the original.
2. Inspect speech text, personal names and every word highlight; a prompt can hint vocabulary without guaranteeing correct recognition.
3. Adjust silence threshold/minimum duration if pauses or word endings are cut too aggressively.
4. Check the burned-in export and editable SRT; choose a larger model only after checking its hardware/download needs.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/mandalerajesh-byte/reelkit/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Minimum RAM/VRAM/storage is not documented. Model downloads and compute depend on the selected model; the README gives about 460 MB for small only.
- **Software:** Python 3.9+ per README; FFmpeg; faster-whisper and selected weights; fonts for the chosen script.
- **Platforms:** The README gives Windows, Mac and Linux FFmpeg installation routes. Native version/architecture acceptance has not been independently established.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/mandalerajesh-byte/reelkit/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/mandalerajesh-byte/reelkit/blob/main/README.md)

- **Weights:** MIT project code. faster-whisper/Whisper conversions, FFmpeg and fonts retain separate terms; a package MIT grant does not clear every font or checkpoint.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. MIT project code. faster-whisper/Whisper conversions, FFmpeg and fonts retain separate terms; a package MIT grant does not clear every font or checkpoint.

### How it uses AI

An open Node CLI generates one neural voice WAV per script line through Miragea, measures line durations, caches unchanged takes and writes frame manifests plus caption/subtitle files for Remotion or HyperFrames. [Source](https://github.com/RTY2345RR/remotion-voiceover/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

An open Node CLI generates one neural voice WAV per script line through Miragea, measures line durations, caches unchanged takes and writes frame manifests plus caption/subtitle files for Remotion or HyperFrames. [Source 1](https://github.com/RTY2345RR/remotion-voiceover/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Revising multi-voice narration without regenerating every line and handing actual audio durations to a code-based video timeline. [Source 1](https://github.com/RTY2345RR/remotion-voiceover/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The repository links an unedited two-voice11.78-second example, its MP4/MP3, captions and frame manifest. Seventeen mocked-engine tests do not independently validate live synthesis or word alignment. [Source 1](https://github.com/RTY2345RR/remotion-voiceover/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Use Node 18.17+ and the official GitHub npx package. Obtain a separate Miragea account/key and configure it locally. Remotion or HyperFrames is a separate rendering environment; ffprobe is only needed for non-WAV output. [Source 1](https://github.com/RTY2345RR/remotion-voiceover/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Use Node 18.17+ and the official GitHub npx package. Obtain a separate Miragea account/key and configure it locally. Remotion or HyperFrames is a separate rendering environment; ffprobe is only needed for non-WAV output.

```sh
npx github:RTY2345RR/remotion-voiceover --help
```


```sh
npx github:RTY2345RR/remotion-voiceover plan voiceover.md
```


```sh
npx github:RTY2345RR/remotion-voiceover generate voiceover.md --hyperframes
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/RTY2345RR/remotion-voiceover/blob/main/README.md)

1. Write scene headings, short lines and permitted voice IDs or authorized reference recordings in Markdown/JSON.
2. Inspect plan first; generate only after selecting service terms and paid access.
3. Load voiceover.json and captions.json into the video project; match the composition fps.
4. Listen for pronunciation and repair drifting captions; split long lines. Editing a line reuses other cached audio.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/RTY2345RR/remotion-voiceover/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Node 18.17+; Miragea service access; separate Remotion/HyperFrames project. Optional ffprobe for MP3/non-WAV.
- **Platforms:** Windows, macOS and Linux documented; exact OS-version support is not specified.

The README’s Apache model-license claims are not a substitute for checking each exact MI-GAN, SAM/SlimSAM, U2Net and OCR conversion/checkpoint. PDF/font/native dependencies retain separate terms. Local browser/native compute and first model downloads; no paid inference service is documented for the selected web operations. [Source](https://github.com/Soap2647/paperwell/blob/master/README.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Editing is line-based without paragraph reflow; rotated/skewed text cannot be edited in place, encrypted PDFs do not open, compression rasterizes pages. Model memory and exact revision rights remain incomplete. No application or model was run.

### How it uses AI

A local PDF/image editor integrates learned MI-GAN/LaMa inpainting, SlimSAM/SAM2 object extraction and PaddleOCR text separation with editable document layers. Telea erasing and booklet imposition are conventional operations, distinct from the AI modes. [Source](https://github.com/Soap2647/paperwell/blob/master/README.md)

### Introduction

A local PDF/image editor integrates learned MI-GAN/LaMa inpainting, SlimSAM/SAM2 object extraction and PaddleOCR text separation with editable document layers. Telea erasing and booklet imposition are conventional operations, distinct from the AI modes. [Source 1](https://github.com/Soap2647/paperwell/blob/master/README.md)

For the web route, obtain the source, run npm install and npm run dev, then open the displayed local HTTP address. Production build writes dist for static hosting. The optional Tauri2 desktop shell has a separate Rust/native build setup. [Source 1](https://github.com/Soap2647/paperwell/blob/master/README.md)

- **Platforms:** Browser route plus documented Windows/macOS/Linux Tauri targets; this source review does not verify each packaged OS route.

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Instrument bleed and separation leakage limit detection; ASR can hallucinate or miss lyrics. Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Generated unseen views are hypotheses. Current issue samples include4090/5090 crashes/freezes and resource pressure; a single owner benchmark is not a compatibility guarantee. New native4DGS is a compact alternative whose results differ from FreeTimeGS in the paper. No app or model was executed.

### How it uses AI

A generative pipeline creates consistent target-view videos from a single-person monocular clip and now supplies native dynamic Gaussian reconstruction plus an interactive viewer. [Source](https://github.com/ant-research/4DAnyone/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/ant-research/4DAnyone/blob/main/docs/4dgs.md)

### Introduction

A generative pipeline creates consistent target-view videos from a single-person monocular clip and now supplies native dynamic Gaussian reconstruction plus an interactive viewer. [Source 1](https://github.com/ant-research/4DAnyone/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/ant-research/4DAnyone/blob/main/docs/4dgs.md)

### What it is good for

Producing a reviewable multiview person reference and an exploratory dynamic splat scene from a consented recording. [Source 1](https://github.com/ant-research/4DAnyone/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/ant-research/4DAnyone/blob/main/docs/4dgs.md)

### Demo & examples

Current documentation adds six reconstruction examples and a viewer. The released reconstruction explicitly does not yet match the paper’s FreeTimeGS visual quality/training speed; the prior Turbo speed ratio applies to denoising, not whole reconstruction. [Source 1](https://github.com/ant-research/4DAnyone/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/ant-research/4DAnyone/blob/main/docs/4dgs.md)

### Install

Clone the official source and initialize its GVHMR submodule. Create the documented Python 3.11 Conda environment and install requirements.txt. Missing models/examples download on first use; review SMPL-X and exact model terms first. Reconstruction additionally installs requirements-4dgs.txt without build isolation; GUI requirements are separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/ant-research/4DAnyone/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/ant-research/4DAnyone/blob/main/docs/4dgs.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Clone the official source and initialize its GVHMR submodule. Create the documented Python 3.11 Conda environment and install requirements.txt. Missing models/examples download on first use; review SMPL-X and exact model terms first. Reconstruction additionally installs requirements-4dgs.txt without build isolation; GUI requirements are separate.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/ant-research/4DAnyone.git
```


```sh
cd 4DAnyone
```


```sh
git submodule update --init third_party/GVHMR
```


```sh
conda create -n 4danyone python=3.11 -y
```


```sh
conda activate 4danyone
```


```sh
pip install -r requirements.txt
```


```sh
pip install --no-build-isolation -r requirements-4dgs.txt
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/ant-research/4DAnyone/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/ant-research/4DAnyone/blob/main/docs/4dgs.md)

1. Use clear1080p+9:16 footage with at least121 frames, one visible full/upper body and no large camera movement.
2. Start with a compact six-view inference job and inspect person, motion and generated unseen views before increasing coverage.
3. Point reconstruct.py at the generated result and a distinct output directory; inspect run/config/training logs and saved checkpoints.
4. Load latest.npz with reconstruct.py view on loopback7861, or render a360-degree orbit; inspect artifacts and consent/rights before reuse.

- **Hardware:** The author reports22 GB CUDA peak and27 seconds per121-frame video on one RTX4090 for an inference setting. Total system RAM/storage and universal reconstruction VRAM requirements are not documented; training batch size can be reduced but this does not prove a lower minimum.
- **Software:** Python 3.11 Conda environment, CUDA-compatible PyTorch/dependencies, initialized GVHMR, selected body/generative models; separate4DGS and optional GUI requirements.
- **Platforms:** A CUDA GPU workflow is documented. The reviewed sources do not establish a Windows/Mac or exact Linux-version support matrix.

## BoxCreator

AI origami, kirigami and paper engineering · AI print separation and prepress authoring · 3D, reconstruction & assets

The repository was created on 2026-11-16. This is its first full guide in this library, not independent validation of a new release.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Creating an original editable packaging concept and a matching folding visualization while keeping print and physical tolerances reviewable.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-16. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/baojiachen0214/BoxCreator/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The maintainer publishes a six-face geometric demonstration with real holes and a folding video. It has no production glue flaps or locks and demonstrates the toolchain rather than automated production structural design. [Source](https://github.com/baojiachen0214/BoxCreator/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/baojiachen0214/BoxCreator/blob/main/docs/acceptance.md)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Use Python 3.10+, Poppler tools, Blender and FFmpeg. Clone the whole repository because the agent guide references its documents. Install the Python package in a virtual environment; Blender uses its own separate Python. Supply a permitted dieline PDF, brief and optional references to the agent. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/baojiachen0214/BoxCreator/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/baojiachen0214/BoxCreator/blob/main/docs/acceptance.md)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** Inspected the titles/states in up to ten recently updated issue/PR records and selected first-hand report bodies; 0 records returned. This bounded sample, owner PRs and repository activity do not establish sustained support, closed blockers or independent reliability. [Source](https://github.com/baojiachen0214/boxcreator/issues)
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** MIT original source and documentation. Blender, FFmpeg, fonts, models/agent services and uploaded dielines/artwork retain separate terms. Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate. [Source](https://github.com/baojiachen0214/BoxCreator/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/baojiachen0214/BoxCreator/blob/main/docs/acceptance.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. The compiler does not automatically understand an arbitrary PDF, solve locks, paper thickness/collisions, convert CMYK or certify PDF/X. Default96 DPI pixel comparisons do not replace vector/print preflight. A consistent net or attractive video does not prove load-bearing strength or a production-ready dieline.

### How it uses AI

An AI design agent interprets a dieline and style brief, authors layered vector artwork and a measured face/hinge specification, then uses deterministic Python and Blender tools for verification and folding/orbit previews. [Source](https://github.com/baojiachen0214/BoxCreator/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/baojiachen0214/BoxCreator/blob/main/docs/acceptance.md)

4. Watch the complete folding/orbit film and reopen the packed project; obtain separate printer preflight and a physical proof before production.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/shanehulbert/ingest-sorter/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** Current v1.10 documentation describes an early-testing Apple Silicon/Resolve21 workflow and a review UI. Neither the screenshots nor estimated provider prices independently verify descriptions, face matches or compatibility. [Source](https://github.com/shanehulbert/ingest-sorter/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/shanehulbert/ingest-sorter/blob/main/sorter/tagger.py)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Use a separate parent folder for the app and its sibling library data. Python 3.9+ and FFmpeg are required; OpenCV adds face/movement features. Start python3 sorter/server.py and open localhost:8765. Optional local AI needs Ollama and a selected vision model; the documented example is qwen2.5vl:7b. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/shanehulbert/ingest-sorter/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/shanehulbert/ingest-sorter/blob/main/sorter/tagger.py)

- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** MIT application. Resolve, OpenCV face models, selected Qwen/other vision weights and Anthropic services retain separate terms; media/identity permissions remain the creator’s responsibility. Local compute with optional separately billed Claude API; a chat subscription does not provide API credits. Resolve licensing and service/model terms remain separate. [Source](https://github.com/shanehulbert/ingest-sorter/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/shanehulbert/ingest-sorter/blob/main/sorter/tagger.py)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. One range per whole clip; no speech transcription or dialogue search. Managed Send moves selects and app metadata wins over later Resolve metadata edits. GPS place lookup sends coordinates to OpenStreetMap; Claude sends sampled frames online and its key is kept locally in the library. Local inference does not imply all networking is absent. Early testing alone is not treated as a specific whole-app instability verdict.

### How it uses AI

A local footage library sends sampled frames to an optional Ollama vision model or Claude for descriptions and keywords, supports optional local face matching, and writes selected clip metadata and a draft timeline into DaVinci Resolve. [Source](https://github.com/shanehulbert/ingest-sorter/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/shanehulbert/ingest-sorter/blob/main/sorter/tagger.py)

### Introduction

A local footage library sends sampled frames to an optional Ollama vision model or Claude for descriptions and keywords, supports optional local face matching, and writes selected clip metadata and a draft timeline into DaVinci Resolve. Preselecting and describing authorized rushes before an edit, with explicit human approval of clip ranges, tags and people matches. [Source 1](https://github.com/shanehulbert/ingest-sorter/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/shanehulbert/ingest-sorter/blob/main/sorter/tagger.py)

Neither the screenshots nor estimated provider prices independently verify descriptions, face matches or compatibility. ### Install

Use a separate parent folder for the app and its sibling library data. Python 3.9+ and FFmpeg are required; OpenCV adds face/movement features. Start python3 sorter/server.py and open localhost:8765. Optional local AI needs Ollama and a selected vision model; the documented example is qwen2.5vl:7b. [Source 1](https://github.com/shanehulbert/ingest-sorter/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/shanehulbert/ingest-sorter/blob/main/sorter/tagger.py)

2. Use a separate parent folder for the app and its sibling library data. Python 3.9+ and FFmpeg are required; OpenCV adds face/movement features. Start python3 sorter/server.py and open localhost:8765. Optional local AI needs Ollama and a selected vision model; the documented example is qwen2.5vl:7b.

```sh
python3 sorter/server.py
```


```sh
ollama pull qwen2.5vl:7b
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/shanehulbert/ingest-sorter/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/shanehulbert/ingest-sorter/blob/main/sorter/tagger.py)

1. Begin with copies and Linked Media to preserve footage locations; Managed Media moves approved clips on Send.
2. Describe a few clips, inspect sampled-frame summaries and correct keywords, identities and one in/out range per clip.
3. Approve selects, press Send and explicitly run Ingest Sorter - Apply Last Send inside the intended Resolve project/bin.
4. Inspect the imported metadata and draft timeline. Keep the sibling library backed up and check paths before using the project on another Mac.

- **Software:** Python 3.9+, FFmpeg, optional OpenCV/ExifTool, Ollama vision model or separately billed Claude, and the proprietary DaVinci Resolve host.
- **Platforms:** macOS only currently: tested by the owner on Apple Silicon with Resolve21; Windows/Linux are not supported by the current Mac-specific picker/script installation.

- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** MIT original code/docs and separately described original demo material. Imported historical modules/samples are not relicensed. Qt, FFmpeg, libopenmpt/libxmp and agent-host terms remain separate. Local composition/rendering plus any chosen AI-agent access. No embedded trained model is supplied; the agent authors composition decisions. [Source](https://github.com/ArthurReboulSalze/ChipLoom/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/ArthurReboulSalze/ChipLoom/blob/main/docs/HERMES_FEEDBACK.md) [Source](https://github.com/ArthurReboulSalze/ChipLoom/blob/main/docs/THIRD_PARTY.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. 2–32-channel MOD and a limited one-sample-per-instrument XM profile; IT/multisample XM and several historical MOD effects/signatures are unsupported. Each render is bounded to120 seconds; a longer valid score can still export. MCP has an SDK smoke test but no documented model-driven application session. Heuristic reports and blind comparison pages are not independent music-quality approval.

### How it uses AI

An AI agent composes and revises structured tracker scores through a Python CLI or optional MCP adapter; deterministic sample recipes, validation and MOD/XM export make the resulting notes and sounds editable. [Source](https://github.com/ArthurReboulSalze/ChipLoom/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/ArthurReboulSalze/ChipLoom/blob/main/docs/HERMES_FEEDBACK.md) [Source](https://github.com/ArthurReboulSalze/ChipLoom/blob/main/docs/THIRD_PARTY.md)

### Introduction

An AI agent composes and revises structured tracker scores through a Python CLI or optional MCP adapter; deterministic sample recipes, validation and MOD/XM export make the resulting notes and sounds editable. [Source 1](https://github.com/ArthurReboulSalze/ChipLoom/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/ArthurReboulSalze/ChipLoom/blob/main/docs/HERMES_FEEDBACK.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/ArthurReboulSalze/ChipLoom/blob/main/docs/THIRD_PARTY.md)

### What it is good for

Writing original chiptune/game-music sketches with owned or newly synthesized samples and inspecting the score together with a tracker user. [Source 1](https://github.com/ArthurReboulSalze/ChipLoom/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/ArthurReboulSalze/ChipLoom/blob/main/docs/HERMES_FEEDBACK.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/ArthurReboulSalze/ChipLoom/blob/main/docs/THIRD_PARTY.md)

### Demo & examples

Three owner-created MOD/XM compositions have listening copies and editable projects. A maintainer-published report describes external Hermes/Debian CLI use, but its raw first-hand report and full logs are not public; it does not establish every desktop, MCP or provider workflow. [Source 1](https://github.com/ArthurReboulSalze/ChipLoom/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/ArthurReboulSalze/ChipLoom/blob/main/docs/HERMES_FEEDBACK.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/ArthurReboulSalze/ChipLoom/blob/main/docs/THIRD_PARTY.md)

### Install

Use a cloned checkout with Python 3.12+ and a virtual environment, then install the package locally. The core has no third-party Python dependencies. Audio renders need FFmpeg with libopenmpt, plus libmp3lame for MP3. Optional Qt desktop/libxmp/MCP dependencies are separate; a package prepared for PyPI is not yet a published PyPI release. [Source 1](https://github.com/ArthurReboulSalze/ChipLoom/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/ArthurReboulSalze/ChipLoom/blob/main/docs/HERMES_FEEDBACK.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/ArthurReboulSalze/ChipLoom/blob/main/docs/THIRD_PARTY.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Use a cloned checkout with Python 3.12+ and a virtual environment, then install the package locally. The core has no third-party Python dependencies. Audio renders need FFmpeg with libopenmpt, plus libmp3lame for MP3. Optional Qt desktop/libxmp/MCP dependencies are separate; a package prepared for PyPI is not yet a published PyPI release.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/ArthurReboulSalze/ChipLoom.git
```


```sh
cd ChipLoom
```


```sh
python -m venv .venv
```


```sh
python -m pip install -e .
```


```sh
python -m tracker4agents demo --song pixel-rush --no-audio
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/ArthurReboulSalze/ChipLoom/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/ArthurReboulSalze/ChipLoom/blob/main/docs/HERMES_FEEDBACK.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/ArthurReboulSalze/ChipLoom/blob/main/docs/THIRD_PARTY.md)

1. Give an agent an original musical brief, plan duration and select a coherent owned or synthesized sample palette.
2. Write and validate a new JSON score, inspect notes/effects and export a MOD or supported XM.
3. Audition short sections, inspect report/lint findings and compare versions at matched loudness; these measurements do not judge musical quality.
4. Keep the editable score and samples. In a live session review proposals before accepting, and export a new listening bundle without overwriting older work.

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Python 3.12+; optional FFmpeg/libopenmpt/libmp3lame for audio, PySide6/libxmp for the desktop and a separate coding-agent host.
- **Platforms:** Windows and Linux standalone builds and documented Debian CLI use; Linux desktop evidence is limited to an X11/PipeWire session without a confirmed physical audio-device test. Mac desktop support is not established.

[Source](https://github.com/apresta/neural_tilde/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/apresta/neural_tilde/blob/main/build.sh) [Source](https://github.com/apresta/neural_tilde/blob/main/CMakeLists.txt) [Source](https://github.com/apresta/neural_tilde/blob/main/neural_tilde.cpp)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Mono processing; latency can be nonzero when rates differ. Quality scaling affects only supporting models. Current source warns that switching to an uninitialized submodel can make a quality change unsafe for the audio thread; owner smooth-switching claims are not a universal artifact-free guarantee. The build script follows the latest cross-toolchain rather than documenting a pinned reproduced toolchain.

### How it uses AI

A Max/MSP external loads actual NAM and AIDA-X neural amplifier models, processes mono audio through NeuralAudio and resamples between model and host rates. It exposes model-load/status and supported quality controls to a patch. [Source](https://github.com/apresta/neural_tilde/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/apresta/neural_tilde/blob/main/build.sh) [Source](https://github.com/apresta/neural_tilde/blob/main/CMakeLists.txt) [Source](https://github.com/apresta/neural_tilde/blob/main/neural_tilde.cpp)

2. Send load with the path to a permitted .nam or AIDA-X .json/.aidax capture; check loaded/error/queued and latency messages.
3. For NAM, prewarm before performance. Query quality_supported and adjust quality only on a model that supports it.
4. Audition bypass/model output and changes at conservative levels, check sample-rate conversion delay, and retain the patch and exact permitted model revision.
