## OpenTimbre local neural instruments

Audio, music & voice · AI assisted audio plugin and DSP authoring

The repository was created on 2026-11-11. This is its first full guide in this library, not independent validation of a new release.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Exploring an editable, deliberately limited learned timbre instrument from owned oud/saz recordings on a trusted single-user machine.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-16. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/panosadamop/OpenTimbre/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** Included demonstration sounds are procedural synthetic fixtures, not proof of a pretrained realistic oud/saz model. Current source implements a conditioned10→96→96→24 harmonic MLP; the older v0.5 manual describes a3→64 baseline, so documentation is not fully synchronized with the implementation. [Source](https://github.com/panosadamop/OpenTimbre/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/panosadamop/OpenTimbre/blob/main/docs/NEURAL-INSTRUMENTS.md) [Source](https://github.com/panosadamop/OpenTimbre/blob/main/docs/LIBRARY-TRAINING-EN.md) [Source](https://github.com/panosadamop/OpenTimbre/blob/main/docs/LOCAL-DEVELOPMENT-EN.md) [Source](https://github.com/panosadamop/OpenTimbre/blob/main/services/ai/neural.py)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Use the current checkout and its documented local or neural-container setup. Main README/user manual require Node 22+, while the local-development guide says Node 20+; use the stricter documented22+ route and keep the inconsistency visible. Local backend development uses Python 3.10+ and separate neural requirements with PyTorch2.3–<3. Configure a private AUTH_SECRET and keep services restricted to trusted local access. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. The network predicts24 harmonic amplitudes, with conditioned note/velocity/time/cents/articulation; some envelopes and articulation effects are still procedural. Unrepresented pitches/techniques are extrapolations, and realistic attacks, noise, transitions or acoustic fidelity are not established. Neural rendering explicitly loads on CPU; no universal RAM/VRAM/storage minimum is documented. Older manuals describe in-memory datasets, while newer library-linked records persist per account; do not assume all jobs, browser storage and training paths are safely isolated for multiuser/public deployment.

### How it uses AI

A local instrument studio trains a small PyTorch harmonic-envelope model from permitted note recordings and renders generated WAVs for sample mapping and composition. Conventional additive demo synthesis and recording meters are separate from this learned route. [Source 1](https://github.com/panosadamop/OpenTimbre/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/panosadamop/OpenTimbre/blob/main/docs/NEURAL-INSTRUMENTS.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/panosadamop/OpenTimbre/blob/main/docs/LIBRARY-TRAINING-EN.md) [Source 4](https://github.com/panosadamop/OpenTimbre/blob/main/docs/LOCAL-DEVELOPMENT-EN.md) [Source 5](https://github.com/panosadamop/OpenTimbre/blob/main/services/ai/neural.py)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Use the current checkout and its documented local or neural-container setup. Main README/user manual require Node 22+, while the local-development guide says Node 20+; use the stricter documented22+ route and keep the inconsistency visible. Local backend development uses Python 3.10+ and separate neural requirements with PyTorch2.3–<3. Configure a private AUTH_SECRET and keep services restricted to trusted local access.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/panosadamop/OpenTimbre.git
```


```sh
cd OpenTimbre
```


```sh
docker compose -f docker-compose.neural.yml up --build
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/panosadamop/OpenTimbre/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/panosadamop/OpenTimbre/blob/main/docs/NEURAL-INSTRUMENTS.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/panosadamop/OpenTimbre/blob/main/docs/LIBRARY-TRAINING-EN.md) [Source 4](https://github.com/panosadamop/OpenTimbre/blob/main/docs/LOCAL-DEVELOPMENT-EN.md) [Source 5](https://github.com/panosadamop/OpenTimbre/blob/main/services/ai/neural.py)

1. Open the local Studio, create the intended account and instrument library, and retain original WAV masters outside browser storage.
2. Upload at least two permitted short uncompressed16/32-bit PCM WAVs with correct MIDI pitch, velocity and rights declaration; link them to the desired oud/saz library.
3. Train the local model and retain its model ID/metadata. Generate a short Local Neural WAV and listen for pitch, decay and timbre errors.
4. Import the reviewed result into the sampler/library and export JSON plus WAV backups. Use Song JSON/WAV where microtonal fidelity matters; basic MIDI does not preserve per-note cents.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/panosadamop/OpenTimbre/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/panosadamop/OpenTimbre/blob/main/docs/NEURAL-INSTRUMENTS.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/panosadamop/OpenTimbre/blob/main/docs/LIBRARY-TRAINING-EN.md) [Source 4](https://github.com/panosadamop/OpenTimbre/blob/main/docs/LOCAL-DEVELOPMENT-EN.md) [Source 5](https://github.com/panosadamop/OpenTimbre/blob/main/services/ai/neural.py)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Node 22+ in the main setup; Python 3.10+ for native backend, PyTorch>=2.3,<3 for neural training, or Docker Compose neural configuration. Correctly labeled owned recordings are required; no realistic pretrained instrument is bundled.
- **Platforms:** Windows PowerShell and macOS/Linux development scripts are documented. They do not establish independently tested training/device compatibility; the selected PyTorch build and browser audio support remain relevant.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/panosadamop/OpenTimbre/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/panosadamop/OpenTimbre/blob/main/README.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/panosadamop/OpenTimbre/blob/main/docs/NEURAL-INSTRUMENTS.md) [Source 4](https://github.com/panosadamop/OpenTimbre/blob/main/docs/LIBRARY-TRAINING-EN.md) [Source 5](https://github.com/panosadamop/OpenTimbre/blob/main/docs/LOCAL-DEVELOPMENT-EN.md) [Source 6](https://github.com/panosadamop/OpenTimbre/blob/main/services/ai/neural.py)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** MIT original software. PyTorch/runtime terms and every supplied recording/instrument pack remain separate; recording ownership is not obtained from the code license. No paid inference service is required by the selected local baseline.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. MIT original software. PyTorch/runtime terms and every supplied recording/instrument pack remain separate; recording ownership is not obtained from the code license. No paid inference service is required by the selected local baseline.
- **Cost:** Local compute/storage and owned recording equipment/material. Public-service security and production hardening remain unfinished; this guide assesses trusted local experimentation and completes an older screened record.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/panosadamop/OpenTimbre/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/panosadamop/OpenTimbre/blob/main/docs/NEURAL-INSTRUMENTS.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/panosadamop/OpenTimbre/blob/main/docs/LIBRARY-TRAINING-EN.md) [Source 4](https://github.com/panosadamop/OpenTimbre/blob/main/docs/LOCAL-DEVELOPMENT-EN.md) [Source 5](https://github.com/panosadamop/OpenTimbre/blob/main/services/ai/neural.py)

### Limitations

The network predicts24 harmonic amplitudes, with conditioned note/velocity/time/cents/articulation; some envelopes and articulation effects are still procedural. Unrepresented pitches/techniques are extrapolations, and realistic attacks, noise, transitions or acoustic fidelity are not established. Neural rendering explicitly loads on CPU; no universal RAM/VRAM/storage minimum is documented. Older manuals describe in-memory datasets, while newer library-linked records persist per account; do not assume all jobs, browser storage and training paths are safely isolated for multiuser/public deployment. [Source 1](https://github.com/panosadamop/OpenTimbre/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/panosadamop/OpenTimbre/blob/main/docs/NEURAL-INSTRUMENTS.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/panosadamop/OpenTimbre/blob/main/docs/LIBRARY-TRAINING-EN.md) [Source 4](https://github.com/panosadamop/OpenTimbre/blob/main/docs/LOCAL-DEVELOPMENT-EN.md) [Source 5](https://github.com/panosadamop/OpenTimbre/blob/main/services/ai/neural.py)

### How it uses AI

An AI agent or OpenAI-compatible model authors a structured song specification. ### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/ProjectFlowmar/project5pi-midi-for-claude/blob/main/README.md)

2. Have the agent author a song JSON with tempo, harmony, parts and arrangement; inspect channel names and notes.

4. Listen and revise manually or through the agent, preserving versions. Export aligned stems for a separate mix if useful.

## Open WebUI image models via llama-swap

Images & design · Video, animation & film · Editing, captions & post-production

First complete guide in this library, including completion of any earlier screened entry; not a claim of a launch today.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Using a configured local image-generation workflow from a chat interface while keeping model-specific graphs and backend choices inspectable.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-16. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/mayerwin/open-webui-llamaswap-image-models/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The owner publishes model-selector screenshots, and an independent user reports several days of successful default image generation with supplied custom workflows. That supports first-hand use, not independently assessed image quality or every later custom/multi-image workflow. [Source](https://github.com/mayerwin/open-webui-llamaswap-image-models/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/mayerwin/open-webui-llamaswap-image-models/issues/2)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Prepare Open WebUI0.5+ and llama-swap with the intended image backend reachable through /upstream/<name>. In the Open WebUI admin Functions panel, create a function, paste the official llamaswap_image_models.py source, save and enable it. Configure its Valves and actual backend model files/workflows. It uses aiohttp/pydantic already bundled by the host; model/backend setup remains separate. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/mayerwin/open-webui-llamaswap-image-models/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/mayerwin/open-webui-llamaswap-image-models/issues/2)
- **Maintenance and support · documented:** Inspected the titles/states in up to ten recently updated issue/PR records and selected first-hand report bodies; 3 records returned. This bounded sample, owner PRs and repository activity do not establish sustained support, closed blockers or independent reliability. [Source](https://github.com/mayerwin/open-webui-llamaswap-image-models/issues)
- **Independent use · verified:** An independent user reports several days of actual default-setup use and working image generation, supplies their custom workflow files, and describes hardcoding/documentation and GPU model-unload limitations. This verifies first-hand use of the default bridge, not all custom nodes, formats or output quality. [Source](https://github.com/mayerwin/open-webui-llamaswap-image-models/issues/2)
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** MIT function code. Open WebUI, llama-swap, ComfyUI/custom nodes, exact image/video checkpoints and any remote provider retain separate terms. No model rights or compute credits are supplied by the function. Local compute/models where configured, plus any chosen remote provider or host obligations. Heavy-model duration and API/storage costs are not universally verified. [Source](https://github.com/mayerwin/open-webui-llamaswap-image-models/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/mayerwin/open-webui-llamaswap-image-models/issues/2)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. One-GPU coordination is configuration-dependent, not an out-of-memory guarantee. An earlier user reported that a busy ComfyUI could defeat unloading; current docs offer a queue-drain/memory-check cmdStop recipe, which was not independently stress-tested here. Sending another prompt in the same chat can abandon an in-flight render. File-store uploads accumulate; inline-base64 fallback can make later chat messages large. Remote backends receive their configured inputs.

### How it uses AI

An Open WebUI manifold function exposes selected ComfyUI or OpenAI-compatible image backends as model-dropdown entries, translates prompts/references into workflow requests and stores returned images/video in the chat file store. [Source](https://github.com/mayerwin/open-webui-llamaswap-image-models/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/mayerwin/open-webui-llamaswap-image-models/issues/2)

### Introduction

An Open WebUI manifold function exposes selected ComfyUI or OpenAI-compatible image backends as model-dropdown entries, translates prompts/references into workflow requests and stores returned images/video in the chat file store. ### Install

Prepare Open WebUI0.5+ and llama-swap with the intended image backend reachable through /upstream/<name>. In the Open WebUI admin Functions panel, create a function, paste the official llamaswap_image_models.py source, save and enable it. Configure its Valves and actual backend model files/workflows. It uses aiohttp/pydantic already bundled by the host; model/backend setup remains separate. In the Open WebUI admin Functions panel, create a function, paste the official llamaswap_image_models.py source, save and enable it. Configure its Valves and actual backend model files/workflows. It uses aiohttp/pydantic already bundled by the host; model/backend setup remains separate.


1. Configure LLAMASWAP_URL and MODELS_JSON using a supported built-in template or an inspected custom API graph, with file names matching the actual server.
2. Select an image-model entry and start with a short text-to-image request; inspect returned media and errors.
3. For image editing, attach permitted references in the expected order and use a suitable image-input workflow; missing numbered inputs deliberately fail.
4. Check GPU coordination and file-store behavior, keep separate chats for parallel requests, and back up outputs before any host-side pruning.

## Mascot Booth

Avatars, digital humans & lip sync · Neural rigging and digital puppetry · Video, animation & film

The repository was created on 2026-11-10. This is its first full guide in this library, not independent validation of a new release.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Making a short talking clip from your own mascot art and permitted recording, or preparing an editable presenter for a larger agent-authored film.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-16. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/zekeandlearn/mascot-booth/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The owner shows pose/mouth examples and reports timing measurements on one85-second Taglish take and selected render-sync tests. Those compare internal/provider timestamps and are not independent phoneme accuracy, cross-language or visual-quality validation. [Source](https://github.com/zekeandlearn/mascot-booth/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/zekeandlearn/mascot-booth/blob/main/ASSETS.md) [Source](https://github.com/zekeandlearn/mascot-booth/blob/main/app/js/listen-worker.js) [Source](https://huggingface.co/Xenova/wav2vec2-base-960h) [Source](https://huggingface.co/onnx-community/whisper-base)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** For a clip, use the official static web app in desktop Chrome/Edge. Supply your own six-pose sheet, correct face boxes and permitted audio. No engine installation is required. A local static copy uses Node 20+ and tools/serve-app.mjs; replace reserved branded assets before hosting/distributing a copy. The optional full-film engine additionally needs Git, FFmpeg and node install.mjs, which installs dependencies/agent skills and downloads headless Chrome. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/zekeandlearn/mascot-booth/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/zekeandlearn/mascot-booth/blob/main/ASSETS.md) [Source](https://github.com/zekeandlearn/mascot-booth/blob/main/app/js/listen-worker.js) [Source](https://huggingface.co/Xenova/wav2vec2-base-960h) [Source](https://huggingface.co/onnx-community/whisper-base)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** MIT code, with separate MIT muxer/OFL font notices. Cap Zeke art, logos/icons and sample voice are all rights reserved and must not become your presenter/product; replace them in hosted/distributed copies. Runtime speech conversions/weights, ElevenLabs, AI-host services and supplied character/audio rights remain separate. Supplied/recorded audio can use local processing after model downloads; optional ElevenLabs uses the owner’s separate account/credits. AI pose creation or an agent host can have separate costs. This completes an older screened entry. [Source](https://github.com/zekeandlearn/mascot-booth/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/zekeandlearn/mascot-booth/blob/main/ASSETS.md) [Source](https://github.com/zekeandlearn/mascot-booth/blob/main/app/js/listen-worker.js) [Source](https://huggingface.co/Xenova/wav2vec2-base-960h) [Source](https://huggingface.co/onnx-community/whisper-base)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Without learned alignment or provided timings, mouth motion follows audio loudness/brightness, which is conventional animation. Owner no-upload claims concern the local processing route: external pose generation and optional ElevenLabs send their specified inputs to those services. The inspected worker downloads speech libraries/models; this was not a complete network/security audit. English/Taglish UI and one Taglish example do not guarantee all-language alignment. Auto-transcription and supplied transcripts still need correction.

### How it uses AI

A browser app uses a downloaded wav2vec2 speech model for letter-level forced alignment, with optional Whisper transcription, to drive a drawn mascot’s mouth shapes. Pose cutting/warping and video encoding are conventional; an optional AI-agent engine authors complete cue-based explainer films. ### Introduction

### First project

[Source 1](https://github.com/zekeandlearn/mascot-booth/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/zekeandlearn/mascot-booth/blob/main/ASSETS.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/zekeandlearn/mascot-booth/blob/main/app/js/listen-worker.js) [Source 4](https://huggingface.co/Xenova/wav2vec2-base-960h) [Source 5](https://huggingface.co/onnx-community/whisper-base)

1. Prepare exactly six separated consistent-scale poses of your own character, using authorized drawn art or a separately chosen AI image host.
2. Import the sheet, correct mouth/eye boxes and optionally supply separately drawn mouth shapes.
3. Upload/record permitted speech and enable Sharper lip sync with a correct transcript where possible; inspect alignments and every mouth/pose transition.
4. Export transparent WebM or background MP4 in a supported browser. For the optional engine, retain presenter assets, cue sheets and editable film code, and inspect the full render rather than relying only on its sync check.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/zekeandlearn/mascot-booth/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/zekeandlearn/mascot-booth/blob/main/ASSETS.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/zekeandlearn/mascot-booth/blob/main/app/js/listen-worker.js) [Source 4](https://huggingface.co/Xenova/wav2vec2-base-960h) [Source 5](https://huggingface.co/onnx-community/whisper-base)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Desktop Chrome/Edge for browser video export; model/library downloads for learned alignment/transcription. Node 20+ for the optional local server/engine; FFmpeg and headless Chrome for full films. Minimum RAM/VRAM, model-cache and total working-storage requirements are not documented.
- **Platforms:** The browser route declares desktop Chrome/Edge export; that does not establish every device/OS combination. The optional Node engine declares Windows, macOS and Linux support; this was not independently reproduced.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/zekeandlearn/mascot-booth/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/zekeandlearn/mascot-booth/blob/main/README.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/zekeandlearn/mascot-booth/blob/main/ASSETS.md) [Source 4](https://github.com/zekeandlearn/mascot-booth/blob/main/app/js/listen-worker.js) [Source 5](https://huggingface.co/Xenova/wav2vec2-base-960h) [Source 6](https://huggingface.co/onnx-community/whisper-base)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** MIT code, with separate MIT muxer/OFL font notices. Cap Zeke art, logos/icons and sample voice are all rights reserved and must not become your presenter/product; replace them in hosted/distributed copies. Runtime speech conversions/weights, ElevenLabs, AI-host services and supplied character/audio rights remain separate.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. MIT code, with separate MIT muxer/OFL font notices. Cap Zeke art, logos/icons and sample voice are all rights reserved and must not become your presenter/product; replace them in hosted/distributed copies. Runtime speech conversions/weights, ElevenLabs, AI-host services and supplied character/audio rights remain separate.
- **Cost:** Supplied/recorded audio can use local processing after model downloads; optional ElevenLabs uses the owner’s separate account/credits. AI pose creation or an agent host can have separate costs. This completes an older screened entry.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/zekeandlearn/mascot-booth/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/zekeandlearn/mascot-booth/blob/main/ASSETS.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/zekeandlearn/mascot-booth/blob/main/app/js/listen-worker.js) [Source 4](https://huggingface.co/Xenova/wav2vec2-base-960h) [Source 5](https://huggingface.co/onnx-community/whisper-base)

### Limitations

Without learned alignment or provided timings, mouth motion follows audio loudness/brightness, which is conventional animation. Owner no-upload claims concern the local processing route: external pose generation and optional ElevenLabs send their specified inputs to those services. The inspected worker downloads speech libraries/models; this was not a complete network/security audit. English/Taglish UI and one Taglish example do not guarantee all-language alignment. Auto-transcription and supplied transcripts still need correction. [Source 1](https://github.com/zekeandlearn/mascot-booth/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/zekeandlearn/mascot-booth/blob/main/ASSETS.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/zekeandlearn/mascot-booth/blob/main/app/js/listen-worker.js) [Source 4](https://huggingface.co/Xenova/wav2vec2-base-960h) [Source 5](https://huggingface.co/onnx-community/whisper-base)

### Get the tool

- [Repository](https://github.com/zekeandlearn/mascot-booth)
- [Documentation](https://github.com/zekeandlearn/mascot-booth/blob/main/README.md)

### mx scanned-score music library · MIT

Converting a permitted printed score into a reviewable editable music-programming sketch, then correcting recognition errors.
A music-programming library integrates separately installed Audiveris optical music recognition: its trained neural glyph classifier contributes to scanned-score recognition before mx imports the MusicXML into editable Elixir music. The sound engine, synthesis and ordinary notation are conventional software.
Full setup, hardware, model-term and output-quality review pending. MIT mx grant read in full. Elixir1.20+, Rust and separate AGPL Audiveris 5.11, Tesseract language data and Poppler for PDFs are documented. The tested scanner runbook is Apple Silicon macOS; other host combinations, model/data and sample/instrument-source rights remain separate. Handwritten scores and guaranteed correct rhythms are outside the documented route.

### Brainrotten original-media pipeline · MIT

ElevenLabs and script providers are separate paid services. Fake-provider tests and a five-video hosted trial do not validate live quality or grant media rights.
